Let's design local search more intelligently:
- Maintain a (sum1) and B = auto(a), current max. Identify top peak lags (say top 20).
- For each top lag k, we want reduce B_k. B_k is sum of products a_i a_{k-i}. The largest product pairs dominate. Reducing one of them helps. We can move mass from one endpoint to a destination that minimizes increase of other peaks. We can compute for a candidate destination d the change in all top peak values analytically using precomputed "impact" vectors.

For moving δ from i to j:
Let v = -e_i + e_j times δ. B' = B + 2δ a*v + δ^2 v*v (where a*v convolution). Change at lag k:
Δ_k = 2δ ( (a * v)_k ). Since v sparse, (a*v)_k = -a_{k-i} + a_{k-j} (where a index valid). plus δ^2 terms: -a? Actually v*v: δ^2(e_j*e_j - e_j*e_i - e_i*e_j + e_i*e_i). Convolution: e_p*e_q has lag p+q. So terms: lag j+j: +δ^2, i+i:+δ^2, lag i+j: -2δ^2. But our B change from full quadratic: B+B? If B computed as convolution linear (ordered), Δ = 2 a*v + v*v. a*v = ∑ a_r v_{k-r}. For k, -δ a_{k-i}+δ a_{k-j}. 1. Fast autocorr:
```
def fast_autocorr(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=len(a)
    size=2*n-1
    m=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)[:size]
```
Use `overwrite`? Could cache transform? For local moves, need B updates. For optimizer, recompute many. Could cache FFT of a? But a changes each step; maybe use `np.fft.rfft`.

FFT for gradient: Need conv(p_ext,a) where p length 2n-1, p_k probability over lags 0..2n-2. For each i, conv_i = ∑_{k} p_k a_{k-i} = (p * a_rev)[i+n-1] as previous. We can implement:
```
def p_a_corr(p,a):
    n=len(a); m=1<<(2*n-1).bit_length()
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,m)
    Ar=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],m)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(P*Ar,m)
    return conv[n-1:2*n-1]
```
Then grad=2*conv. Need confirm orientation: For j=0, ∑_k p_k a_{k} (k valid 0..n-1) = conv at n-1? a_rev[n-1-?]. Previous derivation gave Conv[n-1+j]. Good.

Mirror optimization details:
```
def mirror_opt(a0, time_limit, beta_start=80.0, beta_end=4000.0, beta_growth=1.003, top_frac=0.03, target=0.15, verbose=True, seed=0, random_every=2000):
```
Loop iterations. We need compute p efficiently. Use `current_c` maintained.
- At start: `c = fast_autocorr(a)`; `cur_max=c.max()`.
- Keep `loga = np.log(a)`.
- `lr` baseline maybe target / range. We can adapt: if accepted at factor f, `base_lr *= 1 + 0.1*f?` ; if rejected many, reduce.
Pseudo per iter:
```
if time out break
# maybe anneal beta each iter? beta = min(beta*beta_growth, beta_end) every 20 iterations
mask top from c using top_m
p = softmax(beta*(c_mask-cmax))
g = 2*corr(p,a)
g = g - g.mean()
# scale factor
range_g = g.max()-g.min()
if range_g < 1e-15: random
base = target / range_g
# line search factors [1,0.5,0.25,0.125]
accepted=False
for f in factors:
   z = loga - base*f*g
   z -= z.max()
   a_try = exp(z); normalize
   if not finite continue
   c_try = fast_autocorr(a_try)
   m_try = c_try.max()
   # Use log-sum smooth too? maybe accept if m_try < cur_max - 1e-16 or (m_try < best_m -1e-14)
   if m_try < cur_max - 1e-15:
       accept, set loga, a, c=c_try, cur_max=m_try, base_lr=f*base? maybe f
       if m_try < best_m...
       break
   elif m_try < cur_max + 1e-13 and (eval_smooth(c_try,beta) < eval_smooth(c,beta) - 1e-15): # optional accept smoothed?
       # risk; maybe accept if best? only if m_try <= best_m maybe? skip.
if accepted: reject=0; if factor==1 and m_improve: base_factor*=1.05 else keep.
else:
   reject +=1
   if reject>20:
       # random small anti perturbation in loga
       loga += rng.normal(0, 0.02, n) * maybe structured
       a=norm; c=auto; if cur_max > best_m? keep best
- if beta high and no progress, lower beta.
```
But line search computes c_try at each factor; if first fails, second may. 3 FFTs per iter worst. Could reduce by using approximate current max values to choose factor. Maybe use one step with `base` and if fails reduce by 0.5 outside? Simpler: single factor with adaptive. If rejected, reduce base_lr by 0.5 and no extra FFT? We need c_try to know reject; one FFT. Good. If accepted, maybe increase. We'll do one trial per iter. If no accept for several, random. This gives 2 FFTs/iter (gradient corr + trial autocorr). Good.

Gradient corr uses FFT of p and a. We already have c from autocorr(a). Could we compute p_a corr faster using c? Not directly. Need FFT of p. Could reuse FFT of a? In trial autocorr, we need FFT of a_try. For gradient, we need FFT(p) and FFT(a_rev). We can cache A_rev FFT of current a perhaps while computing c? If we compute c via FFT, we can obtain A = rfft(a, size). We can store A for a? But c and gradient in same iteration; if we maintain c from last trial, we don't have A. Could recompute rfft(a) once and use for c? c = irfft(A*A). So at start of iteration, compute A = rfft(a, m) (one FFT), c = irfft(A*A) (inverse). Then gradient corr uses A_rev? A for a, but correlation uses a_rev, whose FFT is conjugate? Since a real, FFT(a_rev) = conj(FFT(a)) with phase? For length m, reverse not circular? a zero-padded length n; reversing within first n changes FFT: FFT(a[::-1] padded zeros) not simply conj(A). Need separate FFT of a_rev. Could compute with rfft each iteration. Two forward FFTs + one inverse for c + one inverse for corr = 4. But if maintain c, we only need corr forward p and a_rev (2 FFT) and trial autocorr forward a_try + inverse =2 -> total 4. If recompute c, +2. We can avoid c recompute by using trial c when accepted. At first we need c and a_rev FFT. We can write `auto_and_rev_fft(a)` returns c, A_rev. But when a changes, need both. Could implement: each iteration, do trial, if accepted, can compute A_rev for new? We need for gradient next. We can compute trial autocorr using rfft(a_try) (one forward) and not store reverse. To reduce, use a custom correlation that uses A (not reverse) differently? Need ∑ p_k a_{k-j}. This is cross-correlation. If we take FFT of p and a, correlation can be obtained as `irfft(np.fft.fftshift? )`. Let's derive using ordinary FFT without reverse? Let's plan param search:
```
def param_objective(theta, n, basis, Hclip=3.0):
   x precomputed cached? For many evaluations, caching basis matrix for n. We'll precompute basis list for each n.
   h = basis @ theta
   h -= h.mean()
   # Limit dynamic range:
   hr = h.max()-h.min()
   if hr > Hclip: h = (h - h.mean()) * (Hclip/hr)
   q = np.exp(h)
   q += small floor? q = np.maximum(q, 1e-6)
   q /= q.sum()
   return score(q)
```
But for Powell, clipping H introduces nonsmoothness; okay. We can set theta bounds such that range not huge.

Pseudo code:
```
def mirror_opt(a0, time_limit, beta_start=50.0, beta_end=4000.0, top_frac_hi=0.02, top_frac_lo=0.005, target=0.12, verbose=True, seed=1):
    n=len(a0); m=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length(); L=2*n-1
    a=norm_sum1(a0)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, m)[:L]
    cur_max=c.max(); best_m=cur_max; best_a=a.copy()
    loga=np.log(a+1e-300)
    beta=beta_start
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    reject=0
    t=0
    base_factor=1.0
    while time < limit:
       t+=1
       # top mask
       if beta <200: frac=top_frac_hi
       elif beta<1500: frac=(top_frac_hi+top_frac_lo)/2?
       else: frac=top_frac_lo
       top_m=max(30, int(n*frac))
       if top_m < L:
          # partition top top_m
          thresh_idx=np.argpartition(c, L-top_m)[:L-top_m]? Need top. For kth=L-top_m, the elements with index >=kth in sorted? np.argpartition(c, kth)[kth:] gives top top_m unsorted (since kth small? Example c len10, top3, kth=7; partition ensures elements before 7 <=? Actually np.argpartition(a,kth) puts kth element at kth, all before <= a[kth], after >= a[kth]. So a[kth:] are top (length len-kth). For top_m, kth=L-top_m, take partition[c][kth:] true top_m. Use:
          idx = np.argpartition(c, L-top_m)[L-top_m:]
          c_use=np.full(L, -1e300); c_use[idx]=c[idx]
       else: c_use=c
       cmax=c_use.max()
       shifted=beta*(c_use-cmax); clip
       p=np.exp(shifted); p_sum=p.sum()
       if p_sum<=0: p=np.zeros(L); p[np.argmax(c)]=1
       else p/=p_sum
       P=np.fft.rfft(p, m)
       corr=np.fft.irfft(P * A.conj(), m)[:n]
       g=2.0*corr
       g-=g.mean()
       range_g=g.max()-g.min()
       if range_g < 1e-15:
          # random break
       else:
          lr=target*base_factor / range_g
          # cap lr? If lr too small no progress. Maybe max 1e6.
          z=loga - lr*g
          z-=z.max()
          # if z has -inf, okay
          a_try=np.exp(z)
          s=a_try.sum()
          if not finite or s<=0: random lower
          else:
             a_try/=s
             A_try=np.fft.rfft(a_try,m)
             c_try=np.fft.irfft(A_try*A_try,m)[:L]
             m_try=c_try.max()
             tol=1e-15
             if m_try < cur_max - tol or m_try < best_m - tol:
                # accept
                a=a_try; loga=np.log(a) (or z? Since z normalized maybe log a = z - log s? We can compute from a for stability, O(n) okay)
                A=A_try; c=c_try; cur_max=m_try
                if m_try < best_m - tol:
                   best_m=m_try; best_a=a.copy(); reject=0
                # adapt base_factor
                base_factor = min(1.2, base_factor*1.02) if m_try < best_m? else 1.0
                beta = min(beta_end, beta*1.004)
             else:
                reject +=1
                base_factor *= 0.7
                if reject>500:
                   beta=max(10,beta*0.5); base_factor=1.0; random_perturb
```

def perturb_log(loga, n, rng, sigma=0.02):

For move δ from s to d:
- subtract contribution from s: v_s: `2δ*(-a)` at lags s..s+n-1. So newB[s:s+n] -= 2δ*a.
- add from d: newB[d:d+n] += 2δ*a.

But `2a*v` where v has +δ at d, -δ at s: `2a*δ(e_d-e_s)`: (a*e_d) has a at d+k? Convolution a with e_d: lag k = a_{k-d}; slice d:d+n=a. `2a*e_s`: slice s:s+n=a. Thus newB = B + 2δ(a_shift_d - a_shift_s) + δ^2(e_{2d}+e_{2s}-2e_{s+d}). Exactly.
We can evaluate max without creating `v` arrays:
```
newB = B.copy()
newB[d:d+n] += 2δ*a
newB[s:s+n] -= 2δ*a
# special (if indices <L)
newB[2*d] += δ^2
newB[2*s] += δ^2
newB[s+d] -= 2δ^2
m = newB.max()
```
This is O(L) copy plus O(n) slices. Copy length L (13k) is okay. For many candidates, copying 13k*100=1.3M floats ~10MB per round, fine. Could be 1000 candidates 100MB, fine. This is much faster than FFT.

Let's design `apply_move`:
```
def apply_move(a, B, src, dst, delta):
    if delta == 0: return a,B
    two_delta_a = 2.0*delta*a
    B[dst:dst+n] += two_delta_a
    B[src:src+n] -= two_delta_a
    d2=delta*delta
    B[2*dst] += d2
    B[2*src] += d2
    B[src+dst] -= 2*d2
    a[src] -= delta
    a[dst] += delta
    # optional clip tiny negatives
    if a[src] < 1e-18: a[src]=0? But we should keep positive? Nonnegative allowed. For search, avoid zeros to allow future moves? We can set a small floor? If source zero, okay. But mirror needs positive; local can have zeros. We can set if a[src] < 1e-18: a[src]=0. For dest sum unchanged.
    return a,B
```
But if a[src] becomes negative due floating, clip. Since delta <= a[src]*frac, okay.

Evaluation of candidate without modifying B:
```
def move_max_trial(B, a, L, n, src, dst, delta):
    # Use B_view? We need copy.
    nb = B.copy()
    add = 2*delta
    nb[dst:dst+n] += add*a
    nb[src:src+n] -= add*a
    d2=delta*delta
    nb[2*dst] += d2
    nb[2*src] += d2
    nb[src+dst] -= 2*d2
    return nb.max()
```
This allocates `nb` every trial. Could optimize by using preallocated buffer `tmp = B.copy()` in loop? We can have `tmp = B.copy()` and update from B each trial? If we modify tmp, need reset. Basis choices:
```
def make_basis(n, include_sym=True):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    c=[]
    # zero means
    def add(arr): arr=arr-arr.mean(); if std>0 arr/=std; c.append(arr)
    add(np.sin(2*np.pi*x)) # anti
    add(np.cos(np.pi*x)) # anti
    add(x-0.5) # anti
    add(np.sin(4*np.pi*x)) # anti
    add(np.cos(3*np.pi*x)) # anti
    if include_sym:
       add(np.cos(2*np.pi*x))
       add(np.cos(4*np.pi*x))
       add((x-0.5)**2)
       add((x-0.5)**3) # actually anti? (1-x-0.5)^3=-; so anti. Maybe include.
    return np.array(c)
```
Normalize each feature zero mean/unit std to make bounds comparable. Note cosπx and sin2πx etc. Some may be correlated.
For pure anti family, choose first 5 anti.
Parameters θ bounds: We want dynamic range limited. Since features standardized, θ=3 gives range maybe ~10 before Hclip. We can set Hclip=3.5. Bounds anti: [-1.5,1.5] for main, [-1,1] for others. But DE optimum may push. Use Hclip to prevent.
Objective: 
```
def param_score(theta, basis, n, Hclip=3.5):
    h = theta @ basis
    if h is not finite return inf
    h = h - h.mean()
    hr = h.max()-h.min()
    if hr > Hclip: h = h*(Hclip/hr)
    q = np.exp(h)
    q = np.maximum(q, 1e-8)
    q /= q.sum()
    # optional smooth noise no
    m = fast_autocorr(q).max()
    return 2*n*m
```
But `fast_autocorr` every DE eval. 3000 evals * n=6700 FFT maybe maybe 30s. Good. Use `m` not `evaluate_score` for speed (sum1).

If Hclip active, objective nonsmooth, DE okay. Maybe use `polish=True`? DE has local polish `minimize` but overkill. Set `polish=False`.

We need avoid long DE if no scipy. Try fallback.

Could also evaluate candidate shapes from DE: convert theta to q.

def local_search(a0, time_limit, verbose=True, seed=0, recompute_every=100, max_no_improve=2000):
    a=np.asanyarray(a0); n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    a=norm_sum1(a)
    B=fast_autocorr(a)
    best_m=B.max(); best_a=a.copy(); best_B=B.copy()
    rng=...
    t0...
    no_imp=0; accepts=0
    # candidate settings adapt: if n small more.
    top_count = min(80, 5*n)
    peak_sources = 5
    src_pairs = 4
    dst_candidates = 80
    trial_dsts = 15
    fracs = [0.03,0.08,0.15,0.01]
    while time < limit and no_imp < max_no_improve:
        # if accepts>0 and accepts%recompute==0: B=fast_autocorr(a) (sync)
        # top peaks
        part = np.argpartition(B, -top_count)[-top_count:]
        top_idx = part[np.argsort(B[part])[::-1]]
        top_vals = B[top_idx]
        if top_vals[0] < best_m - 1e-15? B current best? Yes.
        # inf for dst: use top_subset = top_idx[:min(30,top_count)]
        subset=len(min(30,len(top_idx)))
        kd = top_idx[:subset, None] - np.arange(n)[None,:]
        mask = (kd>=0)&(kd<n)
        # Need a[np.clip(kd,0,n-1)] but if mask false zero. Could do clip only on valid to avoid O(n*sub)? Using np.where evaluates both branches? It will clip all. Use `kd_clip=np.clip(kd,0,n-1); vals = a[kd_clip][mask]` but need sum per column. Simpler:
        vals = np.zeros((subset,n)); valid indices? `np.take(a, kd_clip, where?)` But assigning false zeros.
        # For performance, loop over subset (30) and fill inf:
        inf = np.zeros(n)
        for kk in top_idx[:subset]:
            kk=int(kk)
            start=max(0, kk-n+1); end=min(n-1, kk)
            # inf[j] += a[kk-j] for j in [start,end]
            inf[start:end+1] += a[kk - (np.arange(start,end+1))]
        cand_dst = np.argsort(inf)[:dst_candidates]
        # sources:
        src_set=[]
        for kk in top_idx[:peak_sources]:
            kk=int(kk); start=max(0,kk-n+1); end=min(n-1,kk); idxs=np.arange(start,end+1)
            prod=a[idxs]*a[kk-idxs]
            # top pairs
            prod_idx=np.argpartition(prod, -src_pairs)[-src_pairs:]
            for pi in prod_idx:
                i=int(idxs[pi]); p=kk-i
                # source candidate choose larger a, and i itself
                if a[i] > a[p]: src_set.append(i); src_set.append(p)
                else: src_set.append(p); src_set.append(i)
            # also max a in support
            src_set.append(int(idxs[np.argmax(a[idxs])]))
        unique src limit 10
        # For each src, generate candidates
        best_trial=None
        # We can restrict for each src to dsts with inf low and maybe not in source's peak? 
        for src in unique:
            if a[src] <= 1e-18: continue
            # choose dsts from cand_dst, skip src, take first trial_dsts
            # To diversify, maybe also choose random 5 if no
            for dst in cand_dst[:trial_dsts]:
                dst=int(dst)
                if dst==src: continue
                for frac in fracs:
                   delta=frac*a[src]
                   if delta<=0: continue
                   # quick bound? The current max at least? We can evaluate exact.
                   nb=B.copy()
                   add=2*delta
                   nb[dst:dst+n] += add*a
                   nb[src:src+n] -= add*a
                   d2=delta*delta
                   nb[2*dst] += d2
                   nb[2*src] += d2
                   nb[src+dst] -= 2*d2
                   m=nb.max()
                   if m < (best_trial[0] if best_trial else inf): best_trial=(m,src,dst,delta)
            # To limit, maybe only first 2 src? We'll do total trials cap.
        # valid if best_trial m < best_m - tol
        if best_trial and best_trial[0] < best_m - 1e-16:
             apply; best_m=...; best_a=...; accepts=0? no_imp=0
             # update a, B
             a[src]-=delta; a[dst]+=delta
             B...
             if a[src]<0: a[src]=0; B? slight.
        else:
             no_imp +=1
             if no_imp % 50 ==0 and verbose? print.
             if no_imp > 500: # maybe random local walks
                 # Try random moves that improve current? Since current=best, none. Perturb and quick local? Instead break to phase manager.
                 break
    # recompute B exact and evaluate score? best_B maybe.
    return best_a, 2*n*best_m (if best_m computed from B)
```

Now, detailed code functions:

```
def norm_sum1(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s <= 1e-15: return np.ones_like(a)/len(a)
   return a/s
```
But if length 0? handle.

```
def fast_autocorr(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   n=a.size
   if n==0: return np.array([])
   L=2*n-1
   m=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
   return np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)[:L]
```
Convolution symmetry may have tiny asym; max okay.

```
def score_normalized(a):
   a=np.asarray(...)
   if a.size==0: return inf
   a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-15 return inf
   a=a/s
   B=fast_autocorr(a)
   if B.size==0: return inf
   return float(2*a.size*float(B.max()))
```

Now, let's scrutinize mirror_opt implementation for numerical and time.

```
def mirror_opt(a0, time_limit, beta_start=30.0, beta_end=3000.0, top_frac_hi=0.03, top_frac_lo=0.006, target=0.15, verbose=True, seed=0, max_time_s=...):
    n=len; L=2n-1; m=1<<(L-1).bit_length()
    a=norm_sum1; A=np.fft.rfft(a,m); c=irfft(A*A,m)[:L]
    cur_max=float(c.max()); best_m=cur_max; best_a=a.copy()
    loga=np.log(a); beta=beta_start; base_factor=1.0; reject=0; it=0; rng
    while time<time_limit:
       it+=1
       # adaptive top_frac
       if beta < 200: frac=top_frac_hi
       elif beta < 1200: frac=(top_frac_hi+top_frac_lo)/2
       else: frac=top_frac_lo
       top_m=max(30, int(n*frac))
       if top_m < L:
          part=np.argpartition(c, L-top_m)
          idx=part[L-top_m:]
          c_use=np.full(L, -1e300); c_use[idx]=c[idx]
       else: c_use=c
       cmax=float(c_use.max())
       shifted=beta*(c_use-cmax)
       shifted=np.clip(shifted,-300,300)
       p=np.exp(shifted)
       psum=p.sum()
       if psum <=0 or not finite: p=zeros; p[argmax(c)]=1
       else p=p/psum
       P=np.fft.rfft(p,m)
       corr=np.fft.irfft(P * A.conj(), m)[:n]
       g=2.0*corr
       g-=g.mean()
       range_g=float(g.max()-g.min())
       if range_g < 1e-15:
          reject +=1
       else:
          lr=target*base_factor / range_g
          lr=min(lr, 1e6)
          z=loga - lr*g
          z-=z.max()
          # If some z too negative, exp underflows to 0, okay. If z has nan, random.
          a_try=np.exp(z)
          s=float(a_try.sum())
          if not finite or s<=0: reject; else:
             a_try /= s
             loga_try = z - np.log(s) # but z maybe -inf for zeros; loga_try stays -inf. OK.
             A_try=np.fft.rfft(a_try,m)
             c_try=np.fft.irfft(A_try*A_try,m)[:L]
             m_try=float(c_try.max())
             tol=1e-15
             if m_try < cur_max - tol or m_try < best_m - tol:
                 a=a_try; loga=loga_try; A=A_try; c=c_try; cur_max=m_try
                 if m_try < best_m - tol:
                    best_m=m_try; best_a=a.copy(); reject=0
                 # adapt
                 if m_try < best_m: base_factor=min(1.5, base_factor*1.05)
                 else: base_factor=1.0
                 beta=min(beta_end, beta*1.005)
             else:
                 reject +=1
                 base_factor=max(0.2, base_factor*0.7)
                 if reject > 300:
                    beta=max(10.0, beta*0.5)
                    base_factor=1.0
                    # try random perturbations
                    improved=False
                    for _ in range(8):
                       sig=0.03*rng.uniform(0.5,2.0)
                       # structured anti
                       xx=np.linspace? But inside mirror, can compute random h using basis? To avoid heavy, use normal + cosπ.
                       xx=np.linspace(0,1,n)
                       h=rng.normal(0,sig,n) + sig*rng.uniform(-1,1)*np.cos(np.pi*xx)
                       z=loga+h; z-=z.max(); at=np.exp(z); ss=at.sum(); at/=ss
                       at=norm_sum1(at)
                       A_t=np.fft.rfft(at,m); c_t=irfft(A_t*A_t,m)[:L]; mt=c_t.max()
                       if mt < best_m - tol or mt < cur_max - tol:
                          a=at; loga=np.log(at); A=A_t; c=c_t; cur_max=mt; improved=True
                          if mt<best_m: update best
                          break
                    if not improved:
                       # reset to best
                       a=best_a.copy(); c=fast? Use A? recompute
                       A=np.fft.rfft(a,m); c=irfft(A*A,m)[:L]; cur_max=c.max(); loga=np.log(a); reject=0
       if it % 500 ==0: print? Maybe too much.
    # final ensure
    # recompute best_true
```
But using `cur_max` as exact max of current a. If we accept a move that is not lower than best but lower than cur_max (when current worse), okay. But we try to maintain current close to best. Reset to best when stuck.

Pseudo code full:
```
def local_search(a0, time_limit, verbose=True, seed=0, recompute_every=80):
    a=norm_sum1(a0); n=len; L=2n-1
    B=fast_autocorr(a)
    cur_obj=float(B.max())
    best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a.copy()
    t0; no_obj_improve=0; no_internal=0; accepts=0
    K_sec = min(5, L)
    top_count = max(20, min(80, n//50))  # 100 for 5k? maybe 80
    peak_sources = 8
    src_pairs = 4
    dst_candidates = 100
    trial_dsts = 20
    fracs = (0.005,0.015,0.04,0.1)
    max_trials = max(200, n//30) # n=10k ->333
    while time<limit and no_internal < 500:
       # if accepts % recompute...
       if accepts > 0 and accepts % recompute_every ==0:
          B=fast_autocorr(a); cur_obj=float(B.max()); if cur_obj<best_obj...; 
       # top peaks
       top_part = np.argpartition(B, -top_count)[-top_count:]
       order = np.argsort(B[top_part])[::-1]
       top_idx = top_part[order]
       top_vals = B[top_idx]
       sec_len = min(5, len(top_idx))
       sec_idx = top_idx[:sec_len]
       cur_sec = float(B[sec_idx].sum())
       # inf over top subset maybe 20
       inf = np.zeros(n)
       inf_len = min(20, len(top_idx))
       for kk in top_idx[:inf_len]:
          kk=int(kk); start=max(0,kk-n+1); end=min(n-1,kk)
          if start<=end:
             jj=np.arange(start,end+1)
             inf[jj] += a[kk-jj]
       cand_dst = np.argsort(inf)[:dst_candidates]
       # sources
       src_candidate = []
       for kk in top_idx[:peak_sources]:
          kk=int(kk); start=...; end=...
          if start>end: continue
          idxs=np.arange(start,end+1)
          prod=a[idxs]*a[kk-idxs]
          # top product pairs
          ppart=np.argpartition(prod, -src_pairs)[-src_pairs:]
          for pi in ppart:
             i=int(idxs[pi]); p=kk-i; if p valid:
                # add i and p, maybe sort by a descending
                if a[i] >= a[p]: src_candidate.append((a[i],i)); src_candidate.append((a[p],p))
                else ...
          # add max a in idx
          i_max=int(idxs[np.argmax(a[idxs])]); src_candidate.append((a[i_max], i_max))
       # unique sort descending a, limit 8
       seen=set; src_list=[]; for val,i in sorted(...,reverse=True): if i not seen
       if no src: break
       best_trial=None; best_obj_trial=inf; best_sec_trial=inf
       trials=0
       for src_val, src in src_list[:8]:
          if a[src] <= 1e-18: continue
          # For dst list, maybe skip if inf high? cand_dst sorted.
          for dst in cand_dst[:trial_dsts]:
             dst=int(dst); if dst==src: continue
             # For fractions; maybe choose delta max not exceeds a[src]
             for frac in fracs:
                if trials >= max_trials: break
                delta = frac*a[src]
                if delta <= 0: continue
                trials +=1
                nb = B.copy()
                add = 2.0*delta
                nb[dst:dst+n] += add*a
                nb[src:src+n] -= add*a
                d2=delta*delta
                nb[2*dst] += d2
                nb[2*src] += d2
                nb[src+dst] -= 2.0*d2
                m = float(nb.max())
                if m < best_trial_obj - 1e-16 or (m <= best_trial_obj + 1e-15 and nb[sec_idx].sum() < best_trial_sec - 1e-18): update best_trial
             if trials>=max_trials: break
          if trials>=max_trials: break
       if best_trial is not None:
          m,src,dst,delta = best_trial
          # accept relative to current lexicographically
          if m < cur_obj - 1e-16 or (m <= cur_obj + 1e-14 and float(nb? we didn't store nb sec sum? We stored sec sum for best_trial; need store best_trial_sec)
          -> store best_sec.
          apply to a,B
          if m < best_obj - 1e-15: best_obj=m; best_a=a.copy(); no_obj=0 else no_obj +=1
          no_internal=0; accepts+=1
       else:
          no_internal +=1
       if verbose and (no_internal>0 and no_internal%200==0): print
    # final recompute best
    B_best=fast_autocorr(best_a); bobj=B_best.max()
    # Also evaluate current if score better
    B_cur=fast_autocorr(a); cobj=B_cur.max()
    if cobj < bobj: best_a=a; bobj=cobj
    score=2*n*bobj
    return norm_sum1(best_a), score
```

```
def optimize_candidate(a0, time_alloc, seed=0, verbose=False):
   t0=time.monotonic()
   a=a0
   # local 20%
   t1=min(time_alloc*0.2, 120.0)
   if t1>10: a, sc = local_search(a, t1, verbose=False, seed=seed)
   # mirror 60%
   rem = time_alloc - (time.monotonic()-t0)
   t2=min(rem*0.6, 900.0)
   if t2>20: a, scm = mirror_opt(a, t2, verbose=False, seed=seed+1)
   # local remainder mostly
   rem = time_alloc - (time.monotonic()-t0)
   t3=max(0.0, rem*0.9)
   if t3>5: a, sc = local_search(a, t3, verbose=False, seed=seed+2)
   # final small local if any
   rem = time_alloc - (time.monotonic()-t0)
   if rem > 5: a, sc = local_search(a, rem*0.8, verbose=False, seed=seed+3)
   # compute score
   a=norm_sum1(a); sc=score_normalized(a)
   return a, sc
```

```
def get_basis(n, mode='mixed'):
    key=(n,mode)
    if key in _basis_cache: return _basis_cache[key]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    feats=[]
    # anti features always
    _add_feature(feats, np.sin(2*np.pi*x))
    _add_feature(feats, np.cos(np.pi*x))
    _add_feature(feats, x-0.5)
    _add_feature(feats, np.sin(4*np.pi*x))
    _add_feature(feats, np.cos(3*np.pi*x))
    if mode == 'mixed':
        _add_feature(feats, np.cos(2*np.pi*x))
        _add_feature(feats, np.cos(4*np.pi*x))
        _add_feature(feats, (x-0.5)**2)
        _add_feature(feats, np.sin(6*np.pi*x)) # anti
    # If R==0 fallback
    basis=np.vstack(feats) if feats else np.zeros((1,n))
    _basis_cache[key]=basis
    return basis
```
`_add_feature` top-level:
```
def _add_feature(feats, arr):
    arr=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64)
    arr=arr-arr.mean()
    sd=float(arr.std())
    if sd > 1e-12:
        arr=arr/sd
    else:
        arr=np.zeros_like(arr)
    # Component clipping to avoid extreme values? Already standardized.
    feats.append(arr)
```
Need ensure max value not huge; standardized Gaussian features max ~3. Good.

`param_to_seq`:
```
def param_to_seq(theta, n, basis, Hclip=3.5):
    theta=np.asarray(theta,dtype=np.float64)
    if theta.size != basis.shape[0]: theta=np.resize(theta,basis.shape[0])
    h=np.tensordot(theta,basis,axes=(0,0))
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)): return default_seq(n)
    h=h-h.mean()
    hr=float(h.max()-h.min())
    if hr > Hclip and hr>1e-12: h=h*(Hclip/hr)
    q=np.exp(h)
    q=np.maximum(q,1e-12)
    return norm_sum1(q)
```
If h range huge but finite, exp may overflow before scaling? We scale based on hr before exp, good. But if theta huge, h may overflow in tensordot. Use try except; if inf return default. Could scale theta first? We can clip theta to [-10,10] before h:
```
theta=np.clip(theta,-10,10)
```
Good.

```
def eval_move(B, a, tmp, sec_idx, src, dst, delta, n):
    tmp[:] = B
    add = 2*delta
    tmp[dst:dst+n] += add*a
    tmp[src:src+n] -= add*a
    d2=delta*delta
    tmp[2*dst] += d2
    tmp[2*src] += d2
    tmp[src+dst] -= 2*d2
    m = float(tmp.max())
    sec = float(tmp[sec_idx].sum())
    return m, sec
```
But `tmp` global buffer. This is top-level helper. It modifies `tmp`. Good.

```
def local_search(a0, time_limit, verbose=True, seed=0, recompute_every=60):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    a=norm_sum1(a0)
    n=a.size
    L=2*n-1
    B=fast_autocorr(a)
    cur_obj=float(B.max())
    best_obj=cur_obj
    best_a=a.copy()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    accepts=0
    no_internal=0
    top_count = int(min(80, max(20, n//40)))
    peak_sources = 8
    src_pairs = 5
    dst_candidates = 25
    quick_pairs = max(60, min(200, n//40))
    fracs = (0.01, 0.03, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50)
    gold_iterations = 8
    obj_tol=1e-15
    near_tol=1e-13
    sec_tol=1e-18
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit and no_internal < 800:
       if accepts > 0 and accepts % recompute_every == 0:
          B=fast_autocorr(a)
          cur_obj=float(B.max())
          if cur_obj < best_obj - obj_tol:
             best_obj=cur_obj; best_a=a.copy()
       # top
       if L <= top_count:
          top_idx = np.argsort(B)[::-1]
       else:
          part=np.argpartition(B, L-top_count)
          top_part=part[L-top_count:]
          top_idx=top_part[np.argsort(B[top_part])[::-1]]
       sec_idx=top_idx[:min(8,len(top_idx))]
       cur_sec=float(B[sec_idx].sum())
       if len(sec_idx)==0: cur_sec=0.0
       # inf
       inf=np.zeros(n)
       inf_len=min(15,len(top_idx))
       for kk in top_idx[:inf_len]:
          kk=int(kk)
          start=max(0, kk-n+1); end=min(n-1, kk)
          if start <= end:
             jj=np.arange(start,end+1)
             inf[jj] += a[kk-jj]
       cand_dst=np.argsort(inf)[:dst_candidates]
       # sources
       src_vals=[]
       for kk in top_idx[:peak_sources]:
          kk=int(kk); start=max(0,kk-n+1); end=min(n-1,kk)
          if start>end: continue
          idxs=np.arange(start,end+1)
          prods=a[idxs]*a[kk-idxs]
          if len(prods) > src_pairs:
             ppart=np.argpartition(prods, L-src_pairs)? Wait prods length varies. Need argpartition(prods, len(prods)-src_pairs)[-src_pairs:]
          else all
          for pi in ppart:
             i=int(idxs[pi]); p=kk-i
             if 0 <= p < n:
                # add both
                if a[i] >= a[p]:
                   src_vals.append((float(a[i]), i))
                   src_vals.append((float(a[p]), p))
                else: ...
          i_max=int(idxs[np.argmax(a[idxs])]); src_vals.append((float(a[i_max]), i_max))
       # unique sort limit 8
       src_list=[]; seen=set()
       # sort src_vals descending by value
       src_vals.sort(key=lambda?) Can't lambda. Top-level key? Define `source_key(item): return item[0]`. Use `src_vals.sort(key=source_key, reverse=True)`.
       for val,i in src_vals:
          if i not in seen and a[i] > 1e-15:
             seen.add(i); src_list.append(i)
             if len(src_list)>=8: break
       if not src_list:
          no_internal += 1
          continue
       tmp=np.empty(L)
       # Build pairs
       pairs=[]; pseen=set()
       for src in src_list:
          if a[src]<=1e-15: continue
          for dst in cand_dst[:15]:
             dst=int(dst)
             if dst == int(src): continue
             tup=(int(src),dst)
             if tup not in pseen:
                pseen.add(tup); pairs.append(tup)
          if len(pairs) >= 160: break
       if not pairs:
          no_internal +=1; continue
       # quick evals
       quick=[]
       quick_frac=0.03
       for src,dst in pairs[:quick_pairs]:
          delta=quick_frac*a[src]
          if delta <= 1e-18: continue
          m,sec=eval_move(B,a,tmp,sec_idx,src,dst,delta,n)
          quick.append((m,sec,src,dst))
       if quick:
          quick.sort()
       # initialize best from current maybe? Use inf
       bm=inf; bs=inf; bsrc=-1; bdst=-1; bd=0.0
       # consider quick and fracs top
       # helper to compare/update cannot be nested. We'll inline.
       # We can evaluate a list of (src,dst) with fracs.
       top_pairs = quick[:25]
       for src,dst in top_pairs:
          for frac in fracs:
             delta=frac*a[src]
             if delta <=1e-18: continue
             m,sec=eval_move(...)
             if m < bm - obj_tol or (m <= bm + near_tol and sec < bs - sec_tol):
                bm=m; bs=sec; bsrc=src; bdst=dst; bd=delta
       # golden top 5
       for src,dst in top_pairs[:5]:
          gm,gs,gd=golden_search_move(B,a,tmp,sec_idx,src,dst,n,gold_iterations)
          if gm < bm - obj_tol or (gm <= bm + near_tol and gs < bs - sec_tol):
             bm=gm; bs=gs; bsrc=src; bdst=dst; bd=gd
       # if bsrc>=0 check current
       if bsrc >=0:
          if bm < cur_obj - obj_tol or (bm <= cur_obj + near_tol and bs < cur_sec - sec_tol):
             # apply
             add=2*bd
             B[bdst:bdst+n] += add*a
             B[bsrc:bsrc+n] -= add*a
             d2=bd*bd
             B[2*bdst] += d2
             B[2*bsrc] += d2
             B[bsrc+bdst] -= 2*d2
             a[bsrc] -= bd
             a[bdst] += bd
             if a[bsrc] < 1e-18: a[bsrc]=0.0
             if a[bdst] < 0: a[bdst]=0.0
             cur_obj=bm
             accepts +=1
             no_internal=0
             if bm < best_obj - obj_tol:
                best_obj=bm; best_a=a.copy()
                if verbose: print(...)
       else:
          no_internal +=1
       if verbose and no_internal > 0 and no_internal % 100 ==0:
          print(...)
    # final recompute
    Bbest=fast_autocorr(best_a); objbest=float(Bbest.max())
    Bcur=fast_autocorr(a); objcur=float(Bcur.max())
    if objcur < objbest - obj_tol:
       best_a=a; best_obj=objcur
    score=2*n*float(best_obj)
    a_out=best_a.copy()
    # If current has same objective, maybe return current to preserve internal? Use if objective difference <= 1e-12 and best length same:
    if abs(objcur - best_obj) <= 1e-12:
       # choose current if it has lower top? Not easy. Return current? Could risk slightly worse due drift but within tol. Maybe keep best_a for safety.
       pass
    return norm_sum1(a_out), score
```

```
def golden_search_move(B,a,tmp,sec_idx,src,dst,n,iterations=8):
    delta_max = a[src] * 0.8
    if delta_max <= 1e-18: return (inf, inf, 0.0)
    low=0.0; high=delta_max
    gr=0.6180339887498949
    d1=high - gr*(high-low)
    d2=low + gr*(high-low)
    f1,s1=eval_move(...d1); f2,s2=eval_move(...d2)
    bm=min(f1,f2); bs=...; bd=...
    # Consider quick endpoints? Maybe evaluate high*0.9? Not necessary; but min could be near high. d2 = 0.618 high, interval leaves 0.382 high. After iterations covers.
    for _ in range(iterations):
       if f1 < f2 - 1e-17:
          high=d2; d2=d1; f2=f1; s2=s1
          d1=high - gr*(high-low)
          if d1 == high: d1 = max(0.0, high - 1e-12)
          f1,s1=eval_move(...d1)
       elif f2 < f1 - 1e-17:
          low=d1; d1=d2; f1=f2; s1=s2
          d2=low + gr*(high-low)
          if d2 == low: d2 = min(high, low + 1e-12)
          f2,s2=eval_move(...d2)
       else:
          newlow = min(d1,d2); newhigh=max(d1,d2)
          if newhigh - newlow < 1e-15*max(1.0, delta_max):
             break
          low=newlow; high=newhigh
          d1=high - gr*(high-low); d2=low + gr*(high-low)
          f1,s1=...; f2,s2=...
       if f1 < bm - 1e-17 or (f1 <= bm + 1e-15 and s1 < bs - 1e-18): update
       if f2 < bm ...
    # evaluate maybe mid for final
    mid=0.5*(low+high); fm,sm=eval_move; update
    return bm,bs,bd
```
Since f convex, this should work. But if f is flat, `else` narrows to middle. Tolerance 1e-17 maybe below B diff? B scale 1e-4; f differences can be 1e-10. Use `1e-16`. Good.

```
def mirror_opt(a0, time_limit, beta_start=30.0, beta_end=4000.0, top_frac_hi=0.03, top_frac_lo=0.006, target=0.12, verbose=True, seed=0):
  t0=time.monotonic()
  a=norm_sum1(a0)
  n=a.size; L=2*n-1; m=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
  floor=1e-12/n
  a=np.maximum(a, floor); a/=a.sum()
  A=np.fft.rfft(a,m); c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)[:L]
  cur_max=float(c.max()); best_m=cur_max; best_a=a.copy()
  loga=np.log(a)
  beta=beta_start; base_factor=1.0; reject=0; it=0
  rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
  while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
    it+=1
    # time slice per iteration? no
    if beta < 200: frac=top_frac_hi
    elif beta < 1200: frac=(top_frac_hi+top_frac_lo)/2
    else: frac=top_frac_lo
    top_m=max(30, int(n*frac))
    if top_m >= L:
       idx=np.arange(L)
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, L-top_m)[L-top_m:]
    vals=c[idx]
    cmax=float(vals.max())
    shifted=beta*(vals-cmax)
    shifted=np.clip(shifted, -300.0, 300.0)
    p=np.zeros(L)
    p[idx]=np.exp(shifted)
    psum=float(p.sum())
    if psum > 0 and np.isfinite(psum):
       p /= psum
    else:
       peak=np.argmax(c); p[peak]=1.0
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,m)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(P * A.conj(), m)[:n]
    g=2.0*corr
    g-=g.mean()
    rg=float(g.max()-g.min())
    if rg <= 1e-15:
       reject += 1
    else:
       lr=target*base_factor / rg
       if not np.isfinite(lr) or lr <=0: lr=1.0
       lr=min(lr, 1e6)
       z=loga - lr*g
       zmax=float(z.max())
       if not np.isfinite(zmax): z=loga.copy()
       else: z=z-zmax
       z=np.maximum(z, -50.0)
       a_try=np.exp(z)
       s=float(a_try.sum())
       if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s):
          a_try /= s
          loga_try = z - np.log(s)
          A_try=np.fft.rfft(a_try,m)
          c_try=np.fft.irfft(A_try*A_try,m)[:L]
          m_try=float(c_try.max())
          if m_try < cur_max - 1e-15 or m_try < best_m - 1e-15:
             a=a_try; A=A_try; c=c_try; loga=loga_try; cur_max=m_try
             if m_try < best_m - 1e-15:
                best_m=m_try; best_a=a.copy(); reject=0
             if m_try < best_m: base_factor=min(1.5, base_factor*1.08)
             else: base_factor=1.0
             beta=min(beta_end, beta*1.005)
          else:
             reject += 1
             base_factor=max(0.2, base_factor*0.7)
             if reject > 250:
                beta=max(10.0, beta*0.5)
                base_factor=1.0
                # perturb best
                improved=False
                for _ in range(6):
                   sig=0.03*rng.uniform(0.5,2.0)
                   xax=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
                   h=rng.normal(0.0, sig, n) + sig*rng.uniform(-1.0,1.0)*np.cos(np.pi*xax)
                   z=loga + h
                   z-=z.max(); z=np.maximum(z,-50.0)
                   at=np.exp(z); ss=float(at.sum()); at/=ss
                   A_t=np.fft.rfft(at,m); c_t=np.fft.irfft(A_t*A_t,m)[:L]; mt=float(c_t.max())
                   if mt < best_m - 1e-15 or mt < cur_max - 1e-15:
                      a=at; loga=np.log(at); A=A_t; c=c_t; cur_max=mt; improved=True
                      if mt < best_m - 1e-15:
                         best_m=mt; best_a=a.copy()
                      break
                if not improved:
                   a=best_a.copy()
                   A=np.fft.rfft(a,m); c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)[:L]
                   cur_max=float(c.max()); loga=np.log(a); reject=0
       if verbose and it % 2000 ==0:
          cur_score=2*n*cur_max; print(...)
  # final
  a=norm_sum1(a); best_a=norm_sum1(best_a)
  sc1=score_normalized(a); sc2=score_normalized(best_a)
  if sc2 <= sc1:
     return best_a, sc2
  return a, sc1
```

def propose_candidate(budget_s=1800.0):
  t0=time.monotonic()
  try: budget=float(budget_s)
  except: budget=1800.0
  if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget<=0: budget=1800.0
  hard_deadline = t0 + max(60.0, budget - 90.0)
  best_seq=None; best_score=inf
  # parent
  try: ...
  if best_seq is None: best_seq=default_sequence(6700); best_score=score_normalized(best_seq)
  pool=[(best_seq,best_score,'best0')]
  # if parent length > 12000, interpolate
  if len(best_seq)>12000: best_seq=interpolate_sequence(best_seq,12000); best_score=score_normalized(best_seq); pool[0]=...
  # long cand
  n_main=len(best_seq)
  n_long = n_main
  if n_main < 10000:
      n_long = int(min(10000, max(n_main+1500, n_main*1.5)))
  elif n_main < 12000:
      n_long = 12000
  if n_long != len(best_seq): add_candidate(pool, interpolate_sequence(best_seq,n_long),'long')
  # random param
  remaining = hard_deadline - time.monotonic()
  if remaining > 60:
      rng=np.random.default_rng(123)
      # choose n_param = n_main if <=10000 else 8000? To save, if n_main>10000, do 5000? But best length maybe 10000; param on 10000 okay.
      n_param = n_main
      if n_param > 10000: n_param = 10000
      if n_param < 4000: n_param = 4000
      max_param_time = min(60.0, max(10.0, remaining*0.15))
      pdeadline = time.monotonic()+max_param_time
      for mode in ['anti','mixed']:
         basis=get_basis(n_param, mode)
         R=basis.shape[0]
         while time.monotonic() < pdeadline:
            theta=rng.uniform(-2.0,2.0,R)
            seq=param_to_seq(theta,n_param,basis)
            sc=score_normalized(seq)
            add_candidate(pool, seq, mode+'rand')
            if len(pool) > 60: break? We can just limit total. But while could many if fast. Use count.
      # maybe DE if time
  # DE
  if sopt is not None and n_main <=10000:
     remaining=hard_deadline - now
     if remaining > 200:
        basis=get_basis(n_main,'mixed')
        R=basis.shape[0]
        bounds=[(-2.0,2.0)]*R
        try:
           res=sopt.differential_evolution(de_objective,bounds,args=(n_main,basis,3.5),maxiter=50,popsize=10,seed=3,tol=1e-5,polish=False,updating='immediate')
           seq=param_to_seq(res.x,n_main,basis)
           add_candidate(pool, seq,'de')
        except Exception: pass
  # sort and maybe keep top 10
  pool.sort(key=candidate_key)
  # remove duplicates? Not needed.
  pool = pool[:10]
  # quick local top 4
  quick_deadline = hard_deadline - 300  # reserve
  for i in range(min(4,len(pool))):
     if time.monotonic() > quick_deadline: break
     a,sc,label=pool[i]
     t_fast = min(40.0, (hard_deadline - time.monotonic())*0.2)
     if t_fast <= 3: break
     a_out, sc_out = local_search(a, t_fast, verbose=False, seed=20+i)
     pool[i]=(a_out,sc_out,label)
     if sc_out < best_score - 1e-12:
        best_seq=a_out; best_score=sc_out; print
     pool.sort(key=candidate_key)
  # deep top 4
  weights=(0.45,0.25,0.15,0.10,0.05,0.05)
  for i in range(min(5,len(pool))):
     remaining=hard_deadline - time.monotonic()
     if remaining < 80: break
     w=weights[i] if i < len(weights) else 0.05
     alloc = remaining*w
     if i==0: alloc=min(alloc, 900.0)
     else: alloc=min(alloc, 400.0)
     if alloc < 20: continue
     a,sc,label=pool[i]
     print phase
     a_out, sc_out = optimize_candidate(a, alloc, seed=100+i)
     if sc_out < best_score - 1e-12:
        best_seq=a_out; best_score=sc_out; print
  # length transfer if time
  remaining=hard_deadline - now
  if remaining > 250 and len(best_seq) < 12000:
     new_n = min(12000, int(len(best_seq)*1.25)+1)
     if new_n <= len(best_seq)+100: new_n = min(12000, len(best_seq)+1500)
     if new_n > len(best_seq):
        a_long=interpolate_sequence(best_seq,new_n)
        t_alloc=min(remaining-60, 400)
        a_out,sc_out=optimize_candidate(a_long,t_alloc,seed=999)
        if sc_out < best_score -1e-12: update
  # final local if time
  remaining=hard_deadline - now
  if remaining > 30:
     a_out,sc_out=local_search(best_seq, min(remaining-20,200), verbose=False, seed=555)
     if sc_out < best_score -1e-12: update
  # final validate
  best_seq=norm_sum1(np.asarray(best_seq,float))
  if len(best_seq)>12000: best_seq=interpolate...
  best_seq=np.nan_to_num(...)
  final_score=score_normalized(best_seq)
  print
  return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```

def parent_perturb_seq(base, theta, basis, Hpert=2.0):
    base=np.asarray(base,float).reshape(-1)
    theta=np.clip(np.asarray(theta,float),-10,10)
    h=np.tensordot(theta,basis,axes=(0,0))
    if not finite: return norm_sum1(base)
    h-=h.mean(); hr=max-min; if hr>Hpert: h*=Hpert/hr
    q=base*np.exp(h)
    q=np.maximum(q,1e-12)
    return norm_sum1(q)
```

```
def norm_sum1(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    return a / s
```
If a size 0, return empty.

`s chay`: `fast_autocorr` if n=0 returns empty.

`interpolate_sequence`: 
```
def interpolate_sequence(f, new_n):
    f=np.asarray(f,float).reshape(-1)
    old_n=f.size
    if new_n<=0: return np.array([],float)
    if old_n==0: return np.ones(new_n)/new_n
    if old_n==new_n: return f.copy()
    s=f.sum()
    if s<=1e-15: return np.ones(new_n)/new_n
    fn=f/s
    old_x=np.linspace(0,1,old_n)
    new_x=np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
    fi=np.interp(new_x, old_x, fn)
    fi=np.maximum(fi,0)
    t=fi.sum()
    if t<=1e-15: return np.ones(new_n)/new_n
    return fi/t
```

`_add_feature`:
```
def _add_feature(feats, arr):
   arr=np.asarray(arr,float)
   if arr.size==0: return
   arr=arr-arr.mean()
   sd=float(np.sqrt(np.mean(arr*arr)))? use arr.std()
   if sd>1e-12: arr=arr/sd
   else: arr=np.zeros_like(arr)
   feats.append(arr)
```

`eval_move`: Ensure `tmp` length at least B. If sec_idx array maybe type int. If empty, sec=0. Use local variables for speed.

```
def eval_move(B, a, tmp, sec_idx, src, dst, delta, n):
    tmp[:] = B
    add = 2.0 * delta
    tmp[dst:dst+n] += add * a
    tmp[src:src+n] -= add * a
    d2 = delta * delta
    tmp[2 * dst] += d2
    tmp[2 * src] += d2
    tmp[src + dst] -= 2.0 * d2
    m = float(np.max(tmp))
    if sec_idx.size:
       sec = float(np.sum(tmp[sec_idx]))
    else: sec=0.0
    return m, sec
```
Potential: `tmp[2*src] += d2` if 2*src index equals a slice that just subtracted, okay. If src,dst ints.

`golden_search_move`: Use `float('inf')`. In eval, if delta==0, we can return B.max, sec. But golden doesn't call 0. At final, we might evaluate mid 0? no.

Need handle if `a[src]` zero. Return inf.

Implementation:
```
def golden_search_move(B,a,tmp,sec_idx,src,dst,n,iterations=8):
    delta_max = 0.8*a[src]
    if delta_max <= 1e-18: return (float('inf'), float('inf'), 0.0)
    low=0.0; high=delta_max; gr=0.618...
    d1=high-gr*(high-low); d2=low+gr*(high-low)
    f1,s1=eval_move(...d1); f2,s2=eval_move(...d2)
    bm=f1; bs=s1; bd=d1
    if f2 < bm -1e-17 or (f2 <= bm +1e-15 and s2 < bs -1e-18): update
    for _ in range(iterations):
       if high-low <= 1e-14*max(1.0, delta_max): break
       if f1 < f2 - 1e-17:
          high=d2; d2=d1; f2=f1; s2=s1
          d1=high-gr*(high-low)
          if d1 < 0: d1=0.0
          if d1 == high: d1=high*0.5
          f1,s1=eval_move...
       elif f2 < f1 -1e-17:
          low=d1; d1=d2; f1=f2; s1=s2
          d2=low+gr*(high-low)
          if d2 > high: d2=high
          if d2 == low: d2=low + (high-low)*0.5
          f2,s2=eval_move...
       else:
          mid=(low+high)*0.5
          half=(high-low)*0.1
          d1=max(low, mid-half); d2=min(high, mid+half)
          f1,s1=...; f2,s2=...
       if f1 < bm -... or ... update; same f2
    mid=0.5*(low+high); fm,sm=eval_move; update
    return bm,bs,bd
```
For convex minimization, if f1 < f2, min lies to left of d2, so high=d2 correct. If f2 < f1, low=d1. Else flat, narrow. Good.

```
def optimize_candidate(a0, time_alloc, seed=0, verbose=False):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    a=norm_sum1(a0)
    sc=score_normalized(a)
    t_local1 = time_alloc * 0.20
    t_local1 = max(0.0, min(t_local1, 120.0))
    if t_local1 > 5.0:
       a, sc = local_search(a, t_local1, verbose=verbose, seed=seed)
    rem = time_alloc - (time.monotonic()-t0)
    t_mir = max(0.0, rem * 0.60)
    t_mir = min(t_mir, 1200.0)
    if t_mir > 15.0:
       a, sc = mirror_opt(a, t_mir, verbose=verbose, seed=seed+1)
    rem = time_alloc - (time.monotonic()-t0)
    t_local2 = max(0.0, rem * 0.90)
    if t_local2 > 5.0:
       a, sc = local_search(a, t_local2, verbose=verbose, seed=seed+2)
    rem = time_alloc - (time.monotonic()-t0)
    if rem > 5.0:
       a, sc = local_search(a, rem*0.8, verbose=False, seed=seed+3)
    a=norm_sum1(a); sc=score_normalized(a)
    return a, sc
```

In `local_search`, `inf[jj] += a[kk - jj]`. `kk - jj` array int. Good.

In `local_search`, `quick` list of tuples (m,sec,src,dst). `quick.sort()` sorts by m then sec then src. Good.

In `local_search`, top_pairs = quick[:25]. If quick contains m inf? eval returns finite. If pairs delta tiny, m finite. Good.
